# project-boards 0.2 Item Operations and 0.3 Issue Operations Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the read-only 0.1 viewer an editor: optimistic project-item edits (move, reorder, every field type, sticky items, error log; release 0.2.0), then issue and item operations (assignees, labels, milestone, title, body, comments, close and reopen, add existing, new issue, drafts, archive, remove; release 0.3.0).

**Architecture:** A new `ops` module holds one `Op` per user action: `Op::apply` changes a `BoardSnapshot` (the store replays pending ops on the confirmed snapshot to build what the user sees) and `ops::send` carries it to GitHub through typed mutations in `github::mutations`. The store keeps two layers, confirmed and pending, and re-applies edits GitHub confirmed in the last five minutes after every poll, because GitHub's item index lags writes by over a minute. The controller owns an `OpQueue` (one op at a time per item, at most four in flight, paused by secondary rate limits); the App checks every key against `model::caps::check`, the single capability matrix, and shows its one-line reason when an operation is not allowed.

**Tech Stack:** Rust 2024 (MSRV 1.88), tokio, reqwest 0.13, graphql_client 0.16 (query validation and variables only), serde_json hand decoding, ratatui 0.30 with crossterm 0.29, time 0.3, insta and wiremock for tests. No new dependencies.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-project-boards-design.md`. Read sections 4–9 and 11 before starting. This plan covers spec section 11's "0.2 Item operations" (Part A) and "0.3 Issue operations" (Part B). The previous plan, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-phase0-and-viewer.md`, shows the conventions this one follows; `docs/phase0-findings.md` finding 0 (index lag) shapes the store.

**Out of scope (0.4):** saving and creating views (`W`), local group-by and sort overrides (`g`, `s`, `U`), visual select and bulk edits (`v`), mouse click, wheel and drag. None of those keys is bound by this plan.

## Global Constraints

- Plugin id `tviles.project-boards`; binary `project-boards`; `platforms = ["linux", "macos"]`; no Windows code paths.
- No default key binding uses a Ctrl chord. `Esc` means back or cancel and never quits; `q` quits; `Ctrl+C` stays the fixed quit.
- GitHub is reached only at `https://api.github.com/graphql`. No telemetry.
- The token is never written to logs, caches or state files. Log lines name operations and ids only, never titles, bodies, comments or field values.
- Types generated by `graphql_client` never leave `crates/queries` and `src/github/`. Everything else uses `model` types. Every new query and mutation is a named operation in `crates/queries/graphql/board.graphql`, listed in `crates/queries/src/lib.rs`'s `operation!` macro so `graphql_client` validates it against `schema.graphql`; responses are decoded by hand from `serde_json::Value`, like the existing queries.
- All cache and state writes go through `fsutil::write_atomic`. The cache holds only the confirmed layer, never pending edits.
- `main`'s version in `Cargo.toml` and `herdr-plugin.toml` is identical and is either a published release or ends in `-dev`.
- Edits are optimistic: what the user sees is the confirmed snapshot with pending edits replayed on top. A failed edit leaves the pending list; there are no inverse patches (spec section 5).
- Edits to the same item are sent one at a time in the order made; edits to different items run concurrently, at most 4 at a time (spec section 7).
- `model::caps::check` is the only place that decides whether an operation is allowed. The UI offers, hints and runs only what it allows.
- Confirmation before: removing an item, closing an issue or pull request, and quitting with edits still sending (spec section 6, Confirmation, plus Decision 16).
- Writes need the classic token scopes `project` (every edit) and `repo` or `public_repo` (issue and pull request edits). Fine-grained tokens report no scopes; nothing is blocked for them up front.
- Live tests that write run only against the testbed in `tests/testbed.toml`, create their own throwaway items, and clean up even when an assertion fails. Fixtures hold invented data only.
- Test-driven throughout; `cargo llvm-cov` line coverage of at least 80% at each release task.

## Executing this plan

- **Sandbox:** run every cargo command with `CARGO_HOME=$TMPDIR/cargo-home` (writes to `~/.cargo` are blocked) and allow `crates.io`, `index.crates.io` and `static.crates.io`. Commands below show it. `gh` and the live suite need the user's token and run outside the sandbox; only Tyler runs them, or the controller with his approval.
- **Plan code is a strong draft, not compiled.** Fix compile and API errors inside the task freely. If a fix changes a name, signature or type listed under the task's **Produces**, update every later task in this plan that uses it, in the same commit, and say so in the commit message. A change to what an existing test asserts needs Tyler's approval; adding an argument to an existing call so it compiles (for example `&ItemMarks::default()`) is not a change to what it asserts.
- **Branch:** work on `editing`. Commit after every task; the controller pushes.
- Steps marked **(ask first)** publish to GitHub; confirm with Tyler before running them.
- Interactive checks ("try it" steps needing a real terminal) cannot run in the sandbox: verify by build and tests, and leave the interactive check to the smoke checklist.

## Decisions

### Rulings from the controller (binding)

1. **`x` stays "clear the local filter"** (user-requested in 0.1). Close and reopen move from the spec's `x` to `X`. Task 1 updates the spec.
2. **Key audit against the shipped keymap (`src/ui/keymap.rs`).** Every spec section 6 key keeps its meaning except `x`/`X`:
   - Free and bound by this plan exactly as the spec says: `<` `>` (move), `J` `K` (reorder), `Space` (action menu), `e` (edit a field), `E` (error log), `R` (retry, inside the error log only) in Part A; `a` (assignees), `t` (labels), `c` (comment), `X` (close or reopen), `n` (new), `C` (convert), `A` (archive or unarchive) in Part B.
   - Already bound with the spec's meaning: `h j k l`, arrows, `Tab`, `Shift+Tab`, `L`, `Enter`, `o`, `f`, `/`, `r`, `?`, `Esc`, `q`.
   - Shipped plugin-only keys, no clash: `B` (switch board), `z` (collapse group), `Home`/`End`, and the detail-only `m` (raw text) and `P` (older comments).
   - `g`, `s`, `W`, `U`, `v` stay unbound until 0.4.
   - Popovers with text entry (field, option, iteration, milestone and repository pickers, the assignee and label multi-select, line inputs, the "add existing" search): every printable key is text, so `j`, `q`, `e` type letters; ↑/↓ move the list, `Enter` applies, `Esc` cancels; in the multi-select `Space` toggles instead of typing.
   - Menus without text entry (the `Space` action menu): `j`/`k` and ↑/↓ move, `Enter` runs the highlighted entry, the key shown next to an entry runs it directly, `Esc` closes; other keys do nothing.
   - The status line always ends with `? help`; the item's edit keys before it are cut with `…` when the pane is narrow.
   - The edit keys work the same in the table, the board and the item detail (user decision).
3. **The spec catches up with 0.1** (Task 1, no code): `f` session-only filter ANDed onto the view's GitHub filter; `x` clears it; board columns hide what the view filter excludes on the column field, never hiding a column that holds items; options sharing a name merge into one column; layout (`L`) remembered per board and view in `state.json`; labels and field values drawn as GitHub-coloured pills; bordered GitHub-style cards (header: state dot, repo `#n`, assignee; wrapped title; field pills from the view's visible fields; labels last); the `gh_user` config option; minimum board column width 34; the `x`/`X` change.
4. **Scopes.** Writes need `project` and `repo` (or `public_repo`). `doctor` reports write capability (Task 20). A missing scope shows the setup message once, as the reason for every write key, instead of failing edits one by one (Tasks 3, 9).
5. **Live tests that write** run only against the testbed, create their own throwaway draft or issue in `tviles/project-boards-testbed`, check results with a direct node lookup (immune to the item index lag), and delete what they made even on failure. They never touch another board. Seed script additions (Task 37) are run by Tyler. The measured query and mutation cost is printed by each live test.
6. **No real user data** in the repository: fixtures and tests use invented titles, logins and numbers; recordings come only from the testbed.
7. **Validation and decoding:** every new operation is validated by `graphql_client` against `schema.graphql`; responses decode by hand.

### User answers (2026-10-12, binding)

- **Remove from project** has no key: it is in the `Space` menu only, with confirmation (as Decision 15 and Task 33 have it).
- **Moves stop at the edges:** `>` on the last option does nothing, `<` on the first does nothing; moves are positional over the shown columns, never wrap and never clear (Decision 12, Task 11). Clearing a field is only via `e`.
- **`RECENT_WINDOW` stays 5 minutes** (Decision 9).
- **The live suite may retitle and close the seeded "Sample pull request"**, restoring it afterwards even on failure (Task 37).
- **`fri` on a Friday means next Friday** (Decision 22).

### Plan-level decisions (flag any you disagree with before execution)

8. **One mutation per value type.** Field values are set through six operations (`SetTextValue`, `SetNumberValue`, `SetDateValue`, `SetSingleSelectValue`, `SetMultiSelectValue`, `SetIterationValue`) with scalar variables, rather than a `ProjectV2FieldValue` input variable, so no `null` keys reach GitHub's "exactly one value" input. The seed script already does this. Likewise title, body and milestone each have their own `updateIssue`/`updatePullRequest`/`updateProjectV2DraftIssue` operation, so setting one never sends `null` for another.
9. **Confirmed edits outrank polls for five minutes (`RECENT_WINDOW`).** Phase 0 finding 0: a write stays invisible to `items(...)` for over a minute. After every poll, full load or view list, the store re-applies the edits GitHub confirmed in the last five minutes. Another person's change to the same field in that window shows after it ends ("last write wins", one window late).
10. **Tolerated errors are kept.** `interpret_body` drops `NOT_FOUND`/`FORBIDDEN` errors that come with data; a mutation whose payload is `null` must still show GitHub's message, so `GraphqlResponse` gains `errors` and `mutations::payload` reports them.
11. **`ops` and `store` depend on each other:** `Op::apply` takes `&mut BoardSnapshot`, and the store replays ops. Both are in this crate; the spec's module table is a layering guide, and neither module sees GraphQL types.
12. **Moves are purely positional over the columns the board shows** (user-confirmed, see "User answers" below): `<`/`>` go to the neighbouring shown column (columns the filter hides are not shown, so they are skipped), never wrap, and never clear a value: they stop at the first and the last option, and "No \<field\>" (drawn last) is never a destination. From "No \<field\>", `<` goes to the column before it. Clearing a field is only possible with `e`. A card whose value names a deleted option sits in "No \<field\>". In the table layout the same columns are used (those a board of this view would show).
13. **Reorder needs an unsorted view** (GitHub's own rule) and stays inside its swimlane (board) or group (table). `K` puts the item before its upper neighbour (after that neighbour's predecessor in project order), `J` after its lower neighbour.
14. **Sticky items** are those edited (or created) during the current visit of the current view. "No longer matches" is shown when the view's latest server list lacks them, or when the client-side estimate (`model::filter::still_matches`: single-select and iteration qualifiers, and archived items without `is:archived`) says so. Leaving the view or `r` clears them.
15. **Remove is allowed wherever archive is** (the capability matrix has no row for it). `ItemContent::Unknown` is treated like a redacted item. A placeholder item (a new item GitHub has not created yet, id `local:N`) allows nothing until it is created.
16. **Quitting with edits still sending asks first** (`y` quits, `n`/`Esc` stays). `B` refuses to switch boards while edits are sending.
17. **A secondary rate limit on an edit** puts it back at the front of the queue and pauses all writes until `retry-after`, with a countdown in the header. It is not a failure.
18. **Closing an issue:** the reason picker ("Close as completed", "Close as not planned") is the confirmation. Closing a pull request asks `y/n`. Reopening asks nothing. "Duplicate" is not offered (it needs a duplicate-of issue).
19. **`e` lists every editable field of the item:** the project's text, number, date, single-select, multi-select and iteration fields, plus Title, Assignees, Labels and Milestone (Part B). Body is in the action menu. Built-ins with no write path (Repository, Reviewers, Parent issue, ...) are not listed.
20. **Candidates** (assignable users, labels, milestones) are the first 100 users, 100 labels and 50 open milestones of the item's repository; a current value outside that list is still shown, checked, so it can be removed. Drafts take assignee candidates from the pane's repository, else the project's first linked repository.
21. **Popover filtering is fuzzy** (the typed characters appear in order, case-insensitively). In the multi-select popover the filter cannot contain spaces, because `Space` toggles.
22. **Date input:** `2026-10-03`, `today`, `tomorrow`, `+3d`, `-2d`, `+1w`, and weekday names (`fri`, `friday`), meaning the next such day after today. An empty input clears the field.
23. **Repository picker:** the pane's repository, then the project's linked repositories, then repositories found by typing `owner/name` (sorted by name), then the user's 100 most recently pushed repositories with write access; each listed once, in that fixed order. Typing `owner/name` that is not listed and pressing `Enter` looks it up.
24. **Add existing:** `Enter` searches when the query changed since the last search, otherwise adds the highlighted result. A pasted issue or pull request URL resolves directly.
25. **New issue and new draft open `$EDITOR`** with an empty file: the first non-empty line is the title; everything after that line is the body, with leading and trailing blank lines trimmed (`ui::editor::title_and_body`). A file with no non-empty line cancels.
26. **"Show archived"** in the action menu toggles `is:archived` in the session filter (`f`), so `x` clears it too.
27. **Editing a body needs the item's body loaded.** If the detail has not loaded it, the key loads it and says "press again". `$EDITOR` is only ever started from a key press, never from a background event, so no keystroke is read by the board while the editor runs.
28. **Cache format 3** (Part B): items carry content node ids, label and assignee ids and the milestone; projects carry linked repositories. Older caches are discarded once.

## Review Focus

The five inputs or conditions the spec implies but no feature test exercises, most likely to bite a person first. Each has a test in the task named.

1. **A poll that left GitHub before an edit was confirmed arrives after it** (GitHub's index lag, finding 0): the edited value must not flip back to the old one, and must give way to the server once the window passes. Tests in Task 5 (`a_confirmed_edit_outranks_a_stale_poll_for_the_window`) and Task 10 (`a_stale_poll_after_a_confirmed_move_keeps_the_card_moved`).
2. **Pressing `>` several times quickly on one card:** each move is computed from what the user sees (pending edits included), so the card walks the shown columns and stops at the last option (never into "No \<field\>"); the ops go out one at a time in order. Tests in Task 11 (`quick_moves_follow_the_shown_columns_and_stop_at_the_last_option`) and Task 7 (`ops_on_one_item_go_one_at_a_time_in_order`).
3. **Quitting or switching boards while edits are still sending:** nothing is lost silently: `q` asks, `B` refuses with a note, `Ctrl+C` still quits. Test in Task 9 (`quitting_with_edits_sending_asks_first`).
4. **Label and user names with spaces, capitals or emoji** ("Good First Issue", "🐛 bug") in the multi-select: typing `good` finds it, `Space` toggles instead of typing, and the current labels are pre-checked by exact name. Tests in Task 15 (`filter_is_case_insensitive_and_space_toggles`) and Task 30 (`labels_with_spaces_and_emoji_are_prechecked_and_found`).
5. **The editor exits with an error, or the user saves an empty file (title, comment, new item) or an unchanged one (title, body):** nothing is sent and the board says why. (An emptied body is a real edit: it clears the body.) Tests in Task 31 (`an_aborted_empty_or_unchanged_edit_sends_nothing`).

```
src/model/caps.rs            capability matrix: OpKind, EditContext, Denial, check, capabilities
src/model/input.rs           date and number input parsing
src/model/refs.rs            (Part B) UserRef, LabelRef, MilestoneRef, RepoRef, CloseReason,
                             ContentId, RepoCandidates, ContentHit
src/model/filter.rs          + still_matches (sticky items)
src/model/field.rs           + Field::value_for_bucket
src/model/item.rs            + ContentFields ids (Part B), Item::content_id
src/ops/mod.rs               Op, OpId, Confirmed, NewItem; Op::apply/item/describe/name
src/ops/send.rs              Op -> GitHub mutation(s) -> Confirmed
src/ops/queue.rs             OpQueue: per-item order, 4 in flight, write pause
src/store/mod.rs             Store trait with confirmed and pending layers; MemoryStore
src/github/mutations.rs      payload helper and every mutation method
src/github/lookups.rs        (Part B) candidates, repositories, search
src/github/transport.rs      + GraphqlResponse.errors, interpret_body_keeping_errors
src/github/fixture.rs        + push_body, set_scopes
src/github/client.rs         + Github::scopes
src/sync/mod.rs, syncer.rs   + OpDone, Access, lookup events; Syncer::mutate and lookups
src/ui/keymap.rs             + edit actions
src/ui/app.rs                + modes, commands, edit state; declares the submodules below
src/ui/app/edit.rs           submit, settle, move, reorder, sticky items, hints, error log
src/ui/app/popups.rs         Popup enum and every popover's key handling
src/ui/app/issue.rs          (Part B) assignees, labels, milestone, editor flows, close,
                             archive, remove, new, add existing, convert
src/ui/popup.rs              ListPicker, line input, their rendering
src/ui/multiselect.rs        checkbox multi-select popover
src/ui/marks.rs              ItemMarks: pending, no-longer-matching, highlighted items
src/ui/editor.rs             (Part B) $EDITOR command, temp file, title/body split
src/ui/chrome.rs             + popovers, confirm line, error log, hints, write status
src/ui/table.rs, board.rs    + marks
src/ui/controller.rs         + OpQueue, Mutate jobs, editor effect, lookups
src/ui/runtime.rs            + Mutate and lookup jobs, editor suspend/resume
src/commands/doctor.rs       + write capability
tests/live_edits.rs          live writes against the testbed (feature `live`)
tests/github_fixtures.rs     + recorded mutation decoding
tests/schema.rs              + every mutation used
scripts/seed-testbed.sh      + milestone v2, label "good first issue" (Part B)
```

### Task 1: Bring the spec in line with 0.1 and the editing rulings

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: the spec text every later task argues from. No code.

- [ ] **Step 1: Session filter (spec section 5, Filtering)**

Replace the bullet

```
- **View filter:** GitHub filter syntax, for example `status:Todo assignee:@me -label:bug`, passed to `items(query:)` so results match the web. Changing it refetches the view's ID list.
```

with

```
- **View filter:** GitHub filter syntax, for example `status:Todo assignee:@me -label:bug`, passed to `items(query:)` so results match the web. Changing it refetches the view's ID list.
- **Session filter (`f`):** a filter typed in herdr is ANDed onto the view's GitHub filter for this session only; it is never saved to GitHub. `x` clears it. A view's ID list fetched with a session filter is left out of the disk cache.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Board layout and remembered layouts (spec section 5, Layouts)**

Replace the bullet

```
- **Board:** one column per option of the view's column field (single-select or iteration), in GitHub's option order, plus a "No \<field\>" column. A group-by field adds horizontal swimlanes.
```

with

```
- **Board:** one column per option of the view's column field (single-select or iteration), in GitHub's option order, plus a "No \<field\>" column. A group-by field adds horizontal swimlanes.
  - Columns the effective filter (view filter plus session filter) excludes on the column field are hidden, because GitHub's API has no hidden-columns setting; a column that holds items is never hidden. Options that share a name show as one column, at the last one's place, as on GitHub.
  - Each card is a bordered box: a header with the state dot, the repository's short name and `#number`, and the first assignee (`+N` for more); the title wrapped to at most three lines; the view's visible fields as pills; labels last. The selected card has a heavy accent border. Columns are at least 34 cells wide.
- **Layout choice:** `L` switches between table and board. The choice is remembered per board and view in the state directory, together with the view's layout on GitHub at the time; when GitHub's layout of that view changes, the choice is dropped with a note.
```

### Task 2: Keep GitHub's error messages and token scopes on every response

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/github/transport.rs`, `src/github/fixture.rs`, `src/github/client.rs`, `src/github/mod.rs`
- Create: `src/github/mutations.rs`

**Interfaces:**

```rust
            .unwrap()

        t.push("Op", json!({"addComment": {"clientMutationId": null}}));

        assert!(payload(&r, "addComment").unwrap().is_object());

        t.push("Op", json!({"addComment": null}));

        assert!(matches!(payload(&r, "addComment"), Err(GithubError::Decode(m)) if m.contains("addComment")));
    }

        let t = Arc::new(FixtureTransport::new());
        let gh = Github::new(t.clone());
        assert_eq!(gh.scopes(), None);
        t.set_scopes(Some(vec!["project".into(), "repo".into()]));

        gh.viewer().await.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(gh.scopes().map(|s| s.len()), Some(2));
    }
```

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct GraphqlResponse {
    pub data: serde_json::Value,
    pub rate: RateInfo,
    /// `X-OAuth-Scopes`, present for classic and OAuth tokens only.
    pub scopes: Option<Vec<String>>,
    /// Errors GitHub sent alongside usable data (NOT_FOUND, FORBIDDEN). A mutation whose
    /// payload is null is explained by these.
    pub errors: Vec<GraphqlErrorEntry>,
}
```

Replace `interpret_body` with the pair:

```rust
/// Turns a GraphQL response body into its data, or the error it describes.
/// Data with only NOT_FOUND/FORBIDDEN errors is kept; those mark nodes the viewer cannot see.
pub fn interpret_body(body: serde_json::Value) -> Result<serde_json::Value, GithubError> {
    interpret_body_keeping_errors(body).map(|(data, _)| data)
}

/// `interpret_body`, also returning the tolerated errors that came with the data.
pub fn interpret_body_keeping_errors(
    body: serde_json::Value,
) -> Result<(serde_json::Value, Vec<GraphqlErrorEntry>), GithubError> {
    let errors: Vec<GraphqlErrorEntry> = match body.get("errors") {
        Some(e) => {
            serde_json::from_value(e.clone()).map_err(|e| GithubError::Decode(e.to_string()))?
        }
        None => Vec::new(),
    };
    if let Some(e) = errors
        .iter()
        .find(|e| e.kind.as_deref() == Some("INSUFFICIENT_SCOPES"))
    {
        return Err(GithubError::InsufficientScopes {
            missing: required_scopes(&e.message),
        });
    }
    let data = body.get("data").cloned().unwrap_or(serde_json::Value::Null);
    if !errors.is_empty() && (data.is_null() || !errors.iter().all(is_tolerable)) {
        return Err(GithubError::Graphql(errors));
    }
    if !errors.is_empty() {
        tracing::debug!(
            count = errors.len(),
            "kept data with tolerable GraphQL errors"
        );
    }
    Ok((data, errors))
}
```

```rust
use crate::github::GraphqlErrorEntry;
use crate::github::transport::interpret_body_keeping_errors;

type Queue = VecDeque<Result<(serde_json::Value, Vec<GraphqlErrorEntry>), GithubError>>;

#[derive(Default)]
pub struct FixtureTransport {
    queues: Mutex<HashMap<String, Queue>>,
    log: Mutex<Vec<GraphqlRequest>>,
    rate: Mutex<RateInfo>,
    scopes: Mutex<Option<Vec<String>>>,
}
```

and the methods (replacing `push`, `push_error`, `push_file`, `queue`):

```rust
    /// Queues `data` as the next response to `operation`.
    pub fn push(&self, operation: &str, data: serde_json::Value) {
        self.queue(operation, Ok((data, Vec::new())));
    }

    pub fn push_error(&self, operation: &str, error: GithubError) {
        self.queue(operation, Err(error));
    }

    /// Queues a full GraphQL body (`{"data": ..., "errors": [...]}`), interpreted the way
    /// the HTTP transport interprets one.
    pub fn push_body(&self, operation: &str, body: serde_json::Value) {
        self.queue(operation, interpret_body_keeping_errors(body));
    }

    /// Queues a recorded file: a full GraphQL body (`{"data": ...}`) or bare data.
    pub fn push_file(&self, operation: &str, path: impl AsRef<Path>) {
        let path = path.as_ref();
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(path)
            .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("fixture {}: {e}", path.display()));
        let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&text).expect("fixture is JSON");
        if value.get("data").is_some() || value.get("errors").is_some() {
            self.push_body(operation, value);
        } else {
            self.push(operation, value);
        }
    }

    /// The `X-OAuth-Scopes` every later response reports.
    pub fn set_scopes(&self, scopes: Option<Vec<String>>) {
        *self.scopes.lock().unwrap() = scopes;
    }

    fn queue(
        &self,
        operation: &str,
        result: Result<(serde_json::Value, Vec<GraphqlErrorEntry>), GithubError>,
    ) {
        self.queues
            .lock()
            .unwrap()
            .entry(operation.to_string())
            .or_default()
            .push_back(result);
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Remember scopes in the client**

In `src/github/client.rs`:

```rust
pub struct Github {
    transport: Arc<dyn Transport>,
    rate: Mutex<RateInfo>,
    scopes: Mutex<Option<Vec<String>>>,
}

impl Github {
    pub fn new(transport: Arc<dyn Transport>) -> Self {
        Self {
            transport,
            rate: Mutex::new(RateInfo::default()),
            scopes: Mutex::new(None),
        }
    }

    /// The classic token scopes the latest response that reported any listed. `None` until
    /// then, and always for fine-grained tokens, which report none.
    pub fn scopes(&self) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
        self.scopes.lock().unwrap().clone()
    }

    pub(crate) async fn run(
        &self,
        request: GraphqlRequest,
    ) -> Result<GraphqlResponse, GithubError> {
        let response = self.transport.execute(request).await?;
        *self.rate.lock().unwrap() = response.rate.clone();
        if response.scopes.is_some() {
            *self.scopes.lock().unwrap() = response.scopes.clone();
        }
        Ok(response)
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the payload helper**

At the top of `src/github/mutations.rs` (above the tests):

```rust
use crate::github::GithubError;
use crate::github::transport::GraphqlResponse;
use serde_json::Value;

/// The payload object of mutation `name` (`data[name]`). GitHub answers a refused mutation
/// with a null payload and, usually, a FORBIDDEN or NOT_FOUND error that the transport
/// tolerated; that error is the one reported, so the user sees GitHub's own words.
pub(crate) fn payload<'a>(response: &'a GraphqlResponse, name: &str) -> Result<&'a Value, GithubError> {
    let p = &response.data[name];
    if p.is_object() {
        return Ok(p);
    }
    if !response.errors.is_empty() {
        return Err(GithubError::Graphql(response.errors.clone()));
    }
    Err(GithubError::Decode(format!("GitHub returned no result for {name}")))
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement the matrix (top of `src/model/caps.rs`)**

```rust
//! The capability matrix (spec section 4): the single source of truth for which operations
//! an item allows. The UI offers, hints and runs only what `check` allows, and shows the
//! `Denial` message for anything else.

use crate::model::ids::ItemId;
use crate::model::item::{ContentState, Item, ItemContent};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum OpKind {
    Move,
    Reorder,
    SetField,
    Archive,
    Unarchive,
    Remove,
    Title,
    Body,
    Assignees,
    Labels,
    Milestone,
    Comment,
    Close,
    Reopen,
    Convert,
}

impl OpKind {
    pub const ALL: [OpKind; 15] = [
        OpKind::Move,
        OpKind::Reorder,
        OpKind::SetField,
        OpKind::Archive,
        OpKind::Unarchive,
        OpKind::Remove,
        OpKind::Title,
        OpKind::Body,
        OpKind::Assignees,
        OpKind::Labels,
        OpKind::Milestone,
        OpKind::Comment,
        OpKind::Close,
        OpKind::Reopen,
        OpKind::Convert,
    ];

    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            OpKind::Move => "move",
            OpKind::Reorder => "reorder",
            OpKind::SetField => "edit a field",
            OpKind::Archive => "archive",
            OpKind::Unarchive => "unarchive",
            OpKind::Remove => "remove from project",
            OpKind::Title => "edit title",
            OpKind::Body => "edit body",
            OpKind::Assignees => "assignees",
            OpKind::Labels => "labels",
            OpKind::Milestone => "milestone",
            OpKind::Comment => "comment",
            OpKind::Close => "close",
            OpKind::Reopen => "reopen",
            OpKind::Convert => "convert to issue",
        }
    }

    /// Operations on the project item itself; every content type, private ones included,
    /// allows these (spec: "move and archive only" for redacted items, plus remove).
    fn item_level(self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, OpKind::Move | OpKind::Archive | OpKind::Remove)
    }
}

/// What the board and the token allow, whatever the item.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Default)]
pub struct EditContext {
    /// `ProjectV2.viewerCanUpdate`.
    pub viewer_can_update: bool,
    /// Classic token scopes from `X-OAuth-Scopes`. `None` for fine-grained tokens and before
    /// the first response: nothing is known, so nothing is blocked up front.
    pub scopes: Option<Vec<String>>,
    /// Scopes GitHub refused an edit for (`INSUFFICIENT_SCOPES`), whatever `scopes` says.
    pub refused: Vec<String>,
}

impl EditContext {
    fn granted(&self, any_of: &[&str]) -> bool {
        let refused = any_of.iter().all(|s| self.refused.iter().any(|r| r == s));
        let listed = self
            .scopes
            .as_ref()
            .is_none_or(|s| s.iter().any(|x| any_of.contains(&x.as_str())));
        listed && !refused
    }

    pub fn has_project_scope(&self) -> bool {
        self.granted(&["project"])
    }

    pub fn has_repo_scope(&self) -> bool {
        self.granted(&["repo", "public_repo"])
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Denial {
    ReadOnly,
    NoProjectScope,
    NoRepoScope,
    Archived,
    NotArchived,
    Creating,
    Private,
    DraftHasNo(OpKind),
    DraftNotClosable,
    NotADraft,
    AlreadyClosed,
    AlreadyOpen,
    Merged,
    StateUnknown,
    /// The item was cached before editing needed its content ids (Part B).
    NeedsRefresh,
}

impl Denial {
    /// The one-line reason shown when a key's operation is not allowed. `key` gives the key
    /// bound to an operation, for hints such as "(C)"; without one the hint is left out.
    pub fn message(&self, key: &dyn Fn(OpKind) -> Option<String>) -> String {
        let hint = |kind: OpKind, text: &str| match key(kind) {
            Some(k) => format!(" ({k}{text})"),
            None => String::new(),
        };
        match self {
            Denial::ReadOnly => "This board is read-only for you".into(),
            Denial::NoProjectScope => {
                "Editing needs the token's project scope: run `gh auth refresh -s project` and reopen the board".into()
            }
            Denial::NoRepoScope => {
                "Editing issues needs the repo scope: run `gh auth refresh -s repo` (or public_repo) and reopen the board".into()
            }
            Denial::Archived => format!(
                "Archived items can only be unarchived or removed{}",
                hint(OpKind::Unarchive, " unarchives")
            ),
            Denial::NotArchived => "Only archived items can be unarchived".into(),
            Denial::Creating => "Still being created on GitHub; try again in a moment".into(),
            Denial::Private => {
                "You can't see this item's content; only move, archive and remove work".into()
            }
            Denial::DraftHasNo(kind) => {
                let what = match kind {
                    OpKind::Labels => "labels",
                    OpKind::Milestone => "milestone",
                    OpKind::Comment => "comments",
                    _ => "such field",
                };
                format!(
                    "Drafts have no {what} — convert to issue first{}",
                    hint(OpKind::Convert, "")
                )
            }
            Denial::DraftNotClosable => format!(
                "Drafts can't be closed — archive{} or remove it instead",
                hint(OpKind::Archive, "")
            ),
            Denial::NotADraft => "Only drafts convert to issues".into(),
            Denial::AlreadyClosed => "Already closed".into(),
            Denial::AlreadyOpen => "Already open".into(),
            Denial::Merged => "Merged pull requests can't be reopened".into(),
            Denial::StateUnknown => "This item's state is unknown; refresh with r".into(),
            Denial::NeedsRefresh => {
                "This item was loaded by an older version; refresh with r to edit it".into()
            }
        }
    }
}

/// A new item that GitHub has not created yet.
pub fn is_placeholder(id: &ItemId) -> bool {
    id.as_str().starts_with("local:")
}

fn board_check(ctx: &EditContext) -> Result<(), Denial> {
    if !ctx.viewer_can_update {
        return Err(Denial::ReadOnly);
    }
    if !ctx.has_project_scope() {
        return Err(Denial::NoProjectScope);
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Whether a new item can be added: a draft or existing item (`needs_repo: false`) needs
/// only the board; a new issue also needs the repo scope.
pub fn check_new(needs_repo: bool, ctx: &EditContext) -> Result<(), Denial> {
    board_check(ctx)?;
    if needs_repo && !ctx.has_repo_scope() {
        return Err(Denial::NoRepoScope);
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Whether `kind` is allowed on `item`; the reason when it is not.
pub fn check(kind: OpKind, item: &Item, ctx: &EditContext) -> Result<(), Denial> {
    use OpKind::*;
    if is_placeholder(&item.id) {
        return Err(Denial::Creating);
    }
    board_check(ctx)?;
    if item.archived {
        return if matches!(kind, Unarchive | Remove) {
            Ok(())
        } else {
            Err(Denial::Archived)
        };
    }
    if kind == Unarchive {
        return Err(Denial::NotArchived);
    }
    if kind.item_level() {
        return Ok(());
    }
    let (state, is_pr) = match &item.content {
        ItemContent::Issue { state, .. } => (*state, false),
        ItemContent::PullRequest { state, .. } => (*state, true),
        ItemContent::Draft { .. } => {
            return match kind {
                Reorder | SetField | Title | Body | Assignees => Ok(()),
                Labels | Milestone | Comment => Err(Denial::DraftHasNo(kind)),
                Close | Reopen => Err(Denial::DraftNotClosable),
                Convert if ctx.has_repo_scope() => Ok(()),
                Convert => Err(Denial::NoRepoScope),
                Move | Archive | Unarchive | Remove => Ok(()),
            };
        }
        ItemContent::Redacted | ItemContent::Unknown { .. } => return Err(Denial::Private),
    };
    match kind {
        Reorder | SetField => return Ok(()),
        Convert => return Err(Denial::NotADraft),
        Close => match state {
            ContentState::Open => {}
            ContentState::Closed | ContentState::Merged => return Err(Denial::AlreadyClosed),
            ContentState::Unknown => return Err(Denial::StateUnknown),
        },
        Reopen => match state {
            ContentState::Closed => {}
            ContentState::Merged if is_pr => return Err(Denial::Merged),
            ContentState::Open => return Err(Denial::AlreadyOpen),
            ContentState::Merged | ContentState::Unknown => return Err(Denial::StateUnknown),
        },
        _ => {}
    }
    if !ctx.has_repo_scope() {
        return Err(Denial::NoRepoScope);
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Every operation `item` allows, in `OpKind::ALL` order.
pub fn capabilities(item: &Item, ctx: &EditContext) -> Vec<OpKind> {
    OpKind::ALL
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|k| check(*k, item, ctx).is_ok())
        .collect()
}
```

```rust
//! One `Op` per user action. `apply` is what the edit does to a board snapshot; the store
//! replays pending ops with it on top of the confirmed board. `send` (ops::send) carries
//! an op to GitHub. Generated GraphQL types never appear here.

use crate::model::{FieldId, FieldValue, Item, ItemId};
use crate::store::BoardSnapshot;
use std::collections::HashMap;

pub type OpId = u64;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Op {
    /// Sets a project field: text, number, date, single-select, multi-select or iteration.
    /// Moving a card between columns is this op on the column field.
    SetField {
        item: ItemId,
        field: FieldId,
        value: FieldValue,
    },
    ClearField {
        item: ItemId,
        field: FieldId,
    },
    /// Puts `item` right after `after` in the project's order; `None` puts it first.
    Reorder {
        item: ItemId,
        after: Option<ItemId>,
    },
}

/// What GitHub answered when an op succeeded.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum Confirmed {
    /// Applied as sent.
    Applied,
    /// Applied; this is the item as GitHub now has it.
    Item(Item),
}

/// At most 40 characters of a title, for messages.
fn short(title: &str) -> String {
    if title.chars().count() <= 40 {
        title.to_string()
    } else {
        format!("{}…", title.chars().take(39).collect::<String>())
    }
}

/// Moves `item` to just after `after` (`None`: first). False when either is missing.
fn move_after(order: &mut Vec<ItemId>, item: &ItemId, after: Option<&ItemId>) -> bool {
    let Some(from) = order.iter().position(|i| i == item) else {
        return false;
    };
    let target = match after {
        None => 0,
        Some(a) if a == item => return false,
        Some(a) => match order.iter().position(|i| i == a) {
            Some(p) if p < from => p + 1,
            Some(p) => p,
            None => return false,
        },
    };
    let id = order.remove(from);
    order.insert(target, id);
    true
}

/// Moves `item` within `list` to where its project rank puts it: before the first item
/// ranked after it. Ids without a rank (not loaded yet) count as last.
fn place_by_rank(list: &mut Vec<ItemId>, item: &ItemId, rank: &HashMap<ItemId, usize>) {
    let (Some(from), Some(&mine)) = (list.iter().position(|i| i == item), rank.get(item)) else {
        return;
    };
    let id = list.remove(from);
    let at = list
        .iter()
        .position(|i| rank.get(i).is_none_or(|&r| r > mine))
        .unwrap_or(list.len());
    list.insert(at, id);
}

fn reorder(snap: &mut BoardSnapshot, item: &ItemId, after: Option<&ItemId>) {
    if !snap.items.contains_key(item) || !move_after(&mut snap.order, item, after) {
        return;
    }
    let rank: HashMap<ItemId, usize> = snap
        .order
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .map(|(i, id)| (id.clone(), i))
        .collect();
    for list in snap.views.values_mut() {
        place_by_rank(&mut list.ids, item, &rank);
    }
}

impl Op {
    pub fn item(&self) -> &ItemId {
        match self {
            Op::SetField { item, .. } | Op::ClearField { item, .. } | Op::Reorder { item, .. } => item,
        }
    }

    /// The op's name for logs, which never carry titles or values.
    pub fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Op::SetField { .. } => "SetField",
            Op::ClearField { .. } => "ClearField",
            Op::Reorder { .. } => "Reorder",
        }
    }

    /// Applies the edit to `snap`. Applying twice is the same as once; an item that is not
    /// in the snapshot is left alone.
    pub fn apply(&self, snap: &mut BoardSnapshot) {
        match self {
            Op::SetField { item, field, value } => {
                if let Some(i) = snap.items.get_mut(item) {
                    i.values.insert(field.clone(), value.clone());
                }
            }
            Op::ClearField { item, field } => {
                if let Some(i) = snap.items.get_mut(item) {
                    i.values.remove(field);
                }
            }
            Op::Reorder { item, after } => reorder(snap, item, after.as_ref()),
        }
    }

    /// A short description for the status line and the error log.
    pub fn describe(&self, snap: Option<&BoardSnapshot>) -> String {
        let title = snap
            .and_then(|s| s.items.get(self.item()))
            .map(|i| short(i.title()))
            .unwrap_or_else(|| "an item".into());
        let field_name = |id: &FieldId| {
            snap.and_then(|s| s.project.field(id))
                .map(|f| f.name.clone())
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "a field".into())
        };
        match self {
            Op::SetField { field, value, .. } => {
                format!("set {} to {} on '{title}'", field_name(field), value.display())
            }
            Op::ClearField { field, .. } => format!("clear {} on '{title}'", field_name(field)),
            Op::Reorder { .. } => format!("reorder '{title}'"),
        }
    }
}
```

### Task 5: The store's confirmed and pending layers

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/store/mod.rs`, `src/ui/controller.rs` (`persist_cache`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Op`, `OpId`, `Confirmed` (Task 4); `BoardSnapshot`, `StoreUpdate` (0.1).
- Produces:
  - `store::RECENT_WINDOW: Duration` (5 minutes); `store::Clock = Arc<dyn Fn() -> Instant + Send + Sync>`; `store::Pending { id: OpId, op: Op }`.
  - `trait Store` gains `confirmed(&self) -> Option<&BoardSnapshot>`, `begin(&mut self, op: Op) -> OpId`, `pending(&self) -> &[Pending]`, `settle_ok(&mut self, id: OpId, confirmed: Confirmed) -> Option<(ItemId, ItemId)>` (a placeholder id and the real one, when an op created an item; always `None` in Part A), `settle_failed(&mut self, id: OpId) -> Option<Op>`. `snapshot()` now returns the confirmed board with pending ops replayed.
  - `MemoryStore::with_clock(snapshot: Option<BoardSnapshot>, clock: Clock) -> Self`; `MemoryStore::new` uses the system clock.
  - The controller's disk cache saves `store.confirmed()`.

```rust
    use crate::model::{FieldId, FieldValue, ItemId, OptionId};
    use crate::ops::{Confirmed, Op};
    use std::sync::Mutex;

    fn status_value(option: &str) -> FieldValue {
        FieldValue::SingleSelect { option_id: OptionId::new(option), name: option.into() }
    }

    fn set(item: &str, option: &str) -> Op {
        Op::SetField { item: ItemId::new(item), field: FieldId::new("F_status"), value: status_value(option) }
    }

    fn status_of(s: Option<&BoardSnapshot>, item: &str) -> String {
        s.unwrap().items[&ItemId::new(item)]
            .value(&FieldId::new("F_status"))
            .map(|v| v.display())
            .unwrap_or_default()
    }

    /// A store over the UI fixture board and a clock the test moves by hand.
    fn layered() -> (MemoryStore, Arc<Mutex<Instant>>) {
        let now = Arc::new(Mutex::new(Instant::now()));
        let read = now.clone();
        let clock: Clock = Arc::new(move || *read.lock().unwrap());
        (MemoryStore::with_clock(Some(crate::ui::fixtures::snapshot()), clock), now)
    }

    /// Item `a` as an old poll reports it: Status Todo.
    fn stale_a() -> crate::model::Item {
        crate::ui::fixtures::items().remove(0)
    }

    #[test]
    fn pending_edits_show_on_top_of_the_confirmed_board() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.snapshot(), "a"), "o_done");
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.confirmed(), "a"), "Todo");
        assert_eq!(s.pending().len(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_failed_edit_leaves_and_a_later_one_on_the_same_field_still_shows() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        let first = s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        let second = s.begin(set("a", "o_prog"));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.snapshot(), "a"), "o_prog");
        assert_eq!(s.settle_failed(first), Some(set("a", "o_done")));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.snapshot(), "a"), "o_prog", "the later edit still replays");
        assert_eq!(s.settle_ok(second, Confirmed::Applied), None);
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.confirmed(), "a"), "o_prog");
        assert!(s.pending().is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_failed_edit_alone_leaves_the_board_as_github_has_it() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        let id = s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        s.settle_failed(id);
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.snapshot(), "a"), "Todo");
        assert_eq!(s.settle_failed(id), None, "settling twice does nothing");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_poll_never_overwrites_an_edit_in_flight() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        s.apply(StoreUpdate::UpsertItems(vec![stale_a()]));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.snapshot(), "a"), "o_done");
    }

    /// Review Focus 1: GitHub's item index lags writes by over a minute.
    #[test]
    fn a_confirmed_edit_outranks_a_stale_poll_for_the_window() {
        let (mut s, now) = layered();
        let id = s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        s.settle_ok(id, Confirmed::Applied);
        s.apply(StoreUpdate::UpsertItems(vec![stale_a()]));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.confirmed(), "a"), "o_done");
        s.apply(StoreUpdate::ReplaceItems(crate::ui::fixtures::items()));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.confirmed(), "a"), "o_done", "a full load too");
        *now.lock().unwrap() += RECENT_WINDOW + Duration::from_secs(1);
        s.apply(StoreUpdate::UpsertItems(vec![stale_a()]));
        assert_eq!(status_of(s.confirmed(), "a"), "Todo", "after the window GitHub wins");
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_returned_item_replaces_the_confirmed_one() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        let id = s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        let mut server = stale_a();
        server.values.insert(FieldId::new("F_status"), status_value("o_done"));
        server.values.insert(FieldId::new("F_prio"), status_value("p2"));
        s.settle_ok(id, Confirmed::Item(server));
        let a = &s.confirmed().unwrap().items[&ItemId::new("a")];
        assert_eq!(a.value(&FieldId::new("F_prio")).map(|v| v.display()).as_deref(), Some("p2"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_snapshot_equals_the_confirmed_board_when_nothing_is_pending() {
        let (mut s, _) = layered();
        assert_eq!(s.snapshot(), s.confirmed());
        let id = s.begin(set("a", "o_done"));
        assert_ne!(s.snapshot(), s.confirmed(), "the pending edit shows");
        s.settle_ok(id, Confirmed::Applied);
        assert_eq!(s.snapshot(), s.confirmed(), "settled: one board again");
    }
```

Replace the `Store` trait and `MemoryStore` in `src/store/mod.rs` (keep `StoreUpdate` and its `summary` as they are):

```rust
use crate::model::{Item, ItemId, Project, ViewId};
use crate::ops::{Confirmed, Op, OpId};
use std::sync::Arc;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

/// How long an edit GitHub confirmed outranks what polls say about it. GitHub's item index
/// lags writes by more than a minute (phase 0 finding 0), so a poll in that window can still
/// carry the old value.
pub const RECENT_WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5 * 60);

/// The time source of a store; tests move it by hand.
pub type Clock = Arc<dyn Fn() -> Instant + Send + Sync>;

/// An edit sent (or about to be sent) and not yet confirmed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct Pending {
    pub id: OpId,
    pub op: Op,
}

/// What the UI reads the board through. A later milestone may move this behind a daemon;
/// callers only see this trait.
pub trait Store: Send {
    /// What the user sees: the confirmed board with the pending edits replayed on top.
    fn snapshot(&self) -> Option<&BoardSnapshot>;
    /// The board as GitHub last reported it, plus the edits it confirmed. The cache saves this.
    fn confirmed(&self) -> Option<&BoardSnapshot>;
    /// Server data. Edits confirmed within `RECENT_WINDOW` are re-applied on top of it.
    fn apply(&mut self, update: StoreUpdate);
    /// Adds an edit to the end of the pending list.
    fn begin(&mut self, op: Op) -> OpId;
    fn pending(&self) -> &[Pending];
    /// GitHub applied edit `id`: it leaves the pending list and enters the confirmed board.
    /// Returns `(placeholder, real)` when the edit created an item (Part B).
    fn settle_ok(&mut self, id: OpId, confirmed: Confirmed) -> Option<(ItemId, ItemId)>;
    /// Edit `id` failed: it leaves the pending list and the view is rebuilt without it.
    fn settle_failed(&mut self, id: OpId) -> Option<Op>;
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
enum Recent {
    Op(Op),
}

pub struct MemoryStore {
    confirmed: Option<BoardSnapshot>,
    pending: Vec<Pending>,
    /// `confirmed` with `pending` replayed; `None` while nothing is pending.
    derived: Option<BoardSnapshot>,
    recent: Vec<(Instant, Recent)>,
    next_id: OpId,
    clock: Clock,
}

impl MemoryStore {
    pub fn new(snapshot: Option<BoardSnapshot>) -> Self {
        Self::with_clock(snapshot, Arc::new(Instant::now))
    }

    pub fn with_clock(snapshot: Option<BoardSnapshot>, clock: Clock) -> Self {
        Self {
            confirmed: snapshot,
            pending: Vec::new(),
            derived: None,
            recent: Vec::new(),
            next_id: 0,
            clock,
        }
    }

    fn rebuild(&mut self) {
        self.derived = match &self.confirmed {
            Some(c) if !self.pending.is_empty() => {
                let mut d = c.clone();
                for p in &self.pending {
                    p.op.apply(&mut d);
                }
                Some(d)
            }
            _ => None,
        };
    }

    /// Re-applies the edits GitHub confirmed within `RECENT_WINDOW`, in the order made,
    /// after server data that may predate them; forgets older ones.
    fn reapply_recent(&mut self) {
        let now = (self.clock)();
        self.recent
            .retain(|(at, _)| now.saturating_duration_since(*at) < RECENT_WINDOW);
        let Some(c) = self.confirmed.as_mut() else {
            return;
        };
        for (_, recent) in &self.recent {
            match recent {
                Recent::Op(op) => op.apply(c),
            }
        }
    }
}

impl Store for MemoryStore {
    fn snapshot(&self) -> Option<&BoardSnapshot> {
        self.derived.as_ref().or(self.confirmed.as_ref())
    }

    fn confirmed(&self) -> Option<&BoardSnapshot> {
        self.confirmed.as_ref()
    }

    fn apply(&mut self, update: StoreUpdate) {
        let from_server = matches!(
            update,
            StoreUpdate::Replace(_)
                | StoreUpdate::UpsertItems(_)
                | StoreUpdate::ReplaceItems(_)
                | StoreUpdate::ViewIds { .. }
        );
        match (update, self.confirmed.as_mut()) {
            (StoreUpdate::Replace(s), _) => self.confirmed = Some(s),
            (StoreUpdate::Project(p), Some(s)) => s.set_project(p),
            (StoreUpdate::Project(p), None) => self.confirmed = Some(BoardSnapshot::new(p)),
            (StoreUpdate::UpsertItems(items), Some(s)) => s.upsert_items(items),
            (StoreUpdate::ReplaceItems(items), Some(s)) => s.replace_items(items),
            (StoreUpdate::ViewIds { view, list }, Some(s)) => s.set_view_ids(view, list),
            (StoreUpdate::ClearViewIds(view), Some(s)) => {
                s.views.remove(&view);
            }
            (StoreUpdate::FetchedAt(t), Some(s)) => s.fetched_at = Some(t),
            (other, None) => tracing::warn!(
                update = %other.summary(),
                "store update before the project was known; ignored"
            ),
        }
        if from_server {
            self.reapply_recent();
        }
        self.rebuild();
    }

    fn begin(&mut self, op: Op) -> OpId {
        self.next_id += 1;
        let id = self.next_id;
        self.pending.push(Pending { id, op });
        self.rebuild();
        id
    }

    fn pending(&self) -> &[Pending] {
        &self.pending
    }

    fn settle_ok(&mut self, id: OpId, confirmed: Confirmed) -> Option<(ItemId, ItemId)> {
        let pos = self.pending.iter().position(|p| p.id == id)?;
        let Pending { op, .. } = self.pending.remove(pos);
        if let Some(c) = self.confirmed.as_mut() {
            if let Confirmed::Item(item) = confirmed {
                c.upsert_items(vec![item]);
            }
            op.apply(c);
        }
        self.recent.push(((self.clock)(), Recent::Op(op)));
        self.rebuild();
        None
    }

    fn settle_failed(&mut self, id: OpId) -> Option<Op> {
        let pos = self.pending.iter().position(|p| p.id == id)?;
        let Pending { op, .. } = self.pending.remove(pos);
        self.rebuild();
        Some(op)
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the operations (append to `crates/queries/graphql/board.graphql`)**

```graphql
# --- Mutations: project field values and positions (0.2) -----------------------------------
# One operation per value type, with scalar variables: GitHub's ProjectV2FieldValue takes
# exactly one value, so no other key may be sent, not even as null. Each returns the item as
# GitHub now has it, which goes straight into the confirmed board: the item index lags
# writes by over a minute (phase 0 finding 0).

mutation SetTextValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $text: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {text: $text}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation SetNumberValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $number: Float!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {number: $number}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation SetDateValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $date: Date!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {date: $date}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation SetSingleSelectValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $option: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {singleSelectOptionId: $option}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation SetMultiSelectValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $options: [String!]!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {multiSelectOptionIds: $options}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation SetIterationValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!, $iteration: String!) {
  updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field, value: {iterationId: $iteration}}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

mutation ClearFieldValue($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $field: ID!) {
  clearProjectV2ItemFieldValue(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, fieldId: $field}) {
    projectV2Item { ...ItemFields }
  }
}

# `after: null` moves the item to the top. The payload lists the project's items in their
# new order (ids only); the live suite checks the move with it.
mutation UpdateItemPosition($project: ID!, $item: ID!, $after: ID) {
  updateProjectV2ItemPosition(input: {projectId: $project, itemId: $item, afterId: $after}) {
    items(first: 100) { nodes { id } }
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the methods (in `src/github/mutations.rs`, above the tests)**

```rust
use crate::github::convert;
use crate::github::queries;
use crate::github::transport::GraphqlRequest;
use crate::github::Github;
use crate::model::*;
use graphql_client::GraphQLQuery;

/// The item under `data[name][key]`.
pub(crate) fn item_payload(response: &GraphqlResponse, name: &str, key: &str) -> Result<Item, GithubError> {
    let p = payload(response, name)?;
    convert::item_from_wire(&p[key])
        .ok_or_else(|| GithubError::Decode(format!("{name} returned no item")))
}

impl Github {
    /// Sets `field` on `item` to `value` (text, number, date, single-select, multi-select or
    /// iteration) and returns the item as GitHub now has it.
    pub async fn set_field_value(
        &self,
        project: &ProjectId,
        item: &ItemId,
        field: &FieldId,
        value: &FieldValue,
    ) -> Result<Item, GithubError> {
        let (project, item, field) = (project.0.clone(), item.0.clone(), field.0.clone());
        let request = match value {
            FieldValue::Text(text) => GraphqlRequest::from_body(queries::SetTextValue::build_query(
                queries::set_text_value::Variables { project, item, field, text: text.clone() },
            )),
            FieldValue::Number(number) => GraphqlRequest::from_body(queries::SetNumberValue::build_query(
                queries::set_number_value::Variables { project, item, field, number: *number },
            )),
            FieldValue::Date(date) => GraphqlRequest::from_body(queries::SetDateValue::build_query(
                queries::set_date_value::Variables { project, item, field, date: date.clone() },
            )),
            FieldValue::SingleSelect { option_id, .. } => GraphqlRequest::from_body(
                queries::SetSingleSelectValue::build_query(queries::set_single_select_value::Variables {
                    project,
                    item,
                    field,
                    option: option_id.0.clone(),
                }),
            ),
            FieldValue::MultiSelect { option_ids, .. } => GraphqlRequest::from_body(
                queries::SetMultiSelectValue::build_query(queries::set_multi_select_value::Variables {
                    project,
                    item,
                    field,
                    options: option_ids.iter().map(|o| o.0.clone()).collect(),
                }),
            ),
            FieldValue::Iteration { iteration_id, .. } => GraphqlRequest::from_body(
                queries::SetIterationValue::build_query(queries::set_iteration_value::Variables {
                    project,
                    item,
                    field,
                    iteration: iteration_id.0.clone(),
                }),
            ),
            other => {
                return Err(GithubError::Decode(format!(
                    "`{}` is not a project field value",
                    other.display()
                )));
            }
        };
        let response = self.run(request).await?;
        item_payload(&response, "updateProjectV2ItemFieldValue", "projectV2Item")
    }

    pub async fn clear_field_value(
        &self,
        project: &ProjectId,
        item: &ItemId,
        field: &FieldId,
    ) -> Result<Item, GithubError> {
        let body = queries::ClearFieldValue::build_query(queries::clear_field_value::Variables {
            project: project.0.clone(),
            item: item.0.clone(),
            field: field.0.clone(),
        });
        let response = self.run(GraphqlRequest::from_body(body)).await?;
        item_payload(&response, "clearProjectV2ItemFieldValue", "projectV2Item")
    }

    /// Puts `item` right after `after` in the project's order; `None` puts it first.
    /// Returns the first 100 item ids in the order GitHub now has.
    pub async fn update_item_position(
        &self,
        project: &ProjectId,
        item: &ItemId,
        after: Option<&ItemId>,
    ) -> Result<Vec<ItemId>, GithubError> {
        let body = queries::UpdateItemPosition::build_query(queries::update_item_position::Variables {
            project: project.0.clone(),
            item: item.0.clone(),
            after: after.map(|a| a.0.clone()),
        });
        let response = self.run(GraphqlRequest::from_body(body)).await?;
        let p = payload(&response, "updateProjectV2ItemPosition")?;
        Ok(convert::nodes(p, "items")
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|n| n["id"].as_str().map(ItemId::new))
            .collect())
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `send` (top of `src/ops/send.rs`)**

```rust
use crate::github::{Github, GithubError};
use crate::model::ProjectId;
use crate::ops::{Confirmed, Op};

/// Sends `op` and reports what GitHub confirmed. Ops on the same item must not overlap; the
/// queue (ops::queue) makes sure of that.
pub async fn send(gh: &Github, project: &ProjectId, op: &Op) -> Result<Confirmed, GithubError> {
    match op {
        Op::SetField { item, field, value } => gh
            .set_field_value(project, item, field, value)
            .await
            .map(Confirmed::Item),
        Op::ClearField { item, field } => gh
            .clear_field_value(project, item, field)
            .await
            .map(Confirmed::Item),
        Op::Reorder { item, after } => gh
            .update_item_position(project, item, after.as_ref())
            .await
            .map(|_order| Confirmed::Applied),
    }
}
```

### Task 7: The op queue

**Files:**
- Create: `src/ops/queue.rs`
- Modify: `src/ops/mod.rs`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `OpId` (Task 4).
- Produces: `ops::queue::{OpQueue, MAX_IN_FLIGHT}` with `OpQueue::new()`, `push(&mut self, id: OpId, item: ItemId)`, `ready(&mut self, now: Instant) -> Vec<OpId>` (marks them running), `finished(&mut self, id: OpId)`, `retry_after(&mut self, id: OpId, until: Instant)`, `paused_until(&self, now: Instant) -> Option<Instant>`, `is_idle(&self) -> bool`, `clear(&mut self)`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests (bottom of `src/ops/queue.rs`)**

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::time::Duration;

    fn item(s: &str) -> ItemId {
        ItemId::new(s)
    }

    /// Review Focus 2.
    #[test]
    fn ops_on_one_item_go_one_at_a_time_in_order() {
        let now = Instant::now();
        let mut q = OpQueue::new();
        q.push(1, item("a"));
        q.push(2, item("a"));
        q.push(3, item("a"));
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [1]);
        assert!(q.ready(now).is_empty(), "the second waits for the first");
        q.finished(1);
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [2]);
        q.finished(2);
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [3]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn different_items_run_together_up_to_the_limit() {
        let now = Instant::now();
        let mut q = OpQueue::new();
        for (id, i) in [(1, "a"), (2, "b"), (3, "c"), (4, "d"), (5, "e"), (6, "a")] {
            q.push(id, item(i));
        }
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [1, 2, 3, 4]);
        assert_eq!(MAX_IN_FLIGHT, 4);
        q.finished(2);
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [5], "6 is on a's queue behind 1");
        q.finished(1);
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [6]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_failed_op_still_releases_its_item() {
        let now = Instant::now();
        let mut q = OpQueue::new();
        q.push(1, item("a"));
        q.push(2, item("a"));
        q.ready(now);
        q.finished(1); // failed or not, the next edit to the item still goes out
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [2]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_rate_limited_op_goes_back_first_and_writes_pause() {
        let now = Instant::now();
        let mut q = OpQueue::new();
        q.push(1, item("a"));
        q.push(2, item("a"));
        q.push(3, item("b"));
        assert_eq!(q.ready(now), [1, 3]);
        let until = now + Duration::from_secs(30);
        q.retry_after(1, until);
        q.finished(3);
        assert_eq!(q.paused_until(now), Some(until));
        assert!(q.ready(now + Duration::from_secs(29)).is_empty());
        assert_eq!(q.ready(until), [1], "1 before 2");
        assert_eq!(q.paused_until(until), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn clear_forgets_everything() {
        let now = Instant::now();
        let mut q = OpQueue::new();
        q.push(1, item("a"));
        q.ready(now);
        q.push(2, item("b"));
        assert!(!q.is_idle());
        q.clear();
        assert!(q.is_idle());
        assert!(q.ready(now).is_empty());
    }
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Implement (top of `src/ops/queue.rs`)**

```rust
//! When each pending edit goes out: edits to one item one at a time in the order made, at
//! most `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` across items (GitHub's secondary rate limit on mutations), and none
//! while a secondary rate limit pauses writes (spec section 7).

use crate::model::ItemId;
use crate::ops::OpId;
use std::collections::{HashSet, VecDeque};
use std::time::Instant;

pub const MAX_IN_FLIGHT: usize = 4;

#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct OpQueue {
    waiting: VecDeque<(OpId, ItemId)>,
    running: Vec<(OpId, ItemId)>,
    paused_until: Option<Instant>,
}

impl OpQueue {
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    pub fn push(&mut self, id: OpId, item: ItemId) {
        self.waiting.push_back((id, item));
    }

    /// The ops to send now, marked running: the first waiting op of every item that has none
    /// running, oldest first, until `MAX_IN_FLIGHT` run. Nothing while writes are paused.
    pub fn ready(&mut self, now: Instant) -> Vec<OpId> {
        if self.paused_until(now).is_some() {
            return Vec::new();
        }
        self.paused_until = None;
        let mut busy: HashSet<ItemId> = self.running.iter().map(|(_, i)| i.clone()).collect();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let mut i = 0;
        while i < self.waiting.len() && self.running.len() < MAX_IN_FLIGHT {
            if busy.contains(&self.waiting[i].1) {
                i += 1;
                continue;
            }
            let (id, item) = self.waiting.remove(i).expect("index in range");
            busy.insert(item.clone());
            self.running.push((id, item));
            out.push(id);
        }
        out
    }

    /// Op `id` finished, whether it succeeded or failed.
    pub fn finished(&mut self, id: OpId) {
        self.running.retain(|(r, _)| *r != id);
    }

    /// Op `id` hit a secondary rate limit: it goes back to the front, and nothing is sent
    /// before `until`.
    pub fn retry_after(&mut self, id: OpId, until: Instant) {
        if let Some(pos) = self.running.iter().position(|(r, _)| *r == id) {
            let entry = self.running.remove(pos);
            self.waiting.push_front(entry);
        }
        self.paused_until = Some(self.paused_until.map_or(until, |p| p.max(until)));
    }

    /// When writes resume, while they are paused.
    pub fn paused_until(&self, now: Instant) -> Option<Instant> {
        self.paused_until.filter(|until| now < *until)
    }

    pub fn is_idle(&self) -> bool {
        self.waiting.is_empty() && self.running.is_empty()
    }

    /// Forgets every op (another board opened).
    pub fn clear(&mut self) {
        *self = Self::default();
    }
}
```

```rust
    /// Sends one edit. The log names the op and the error, never titles or values.
    pub async fn mutate(&self, id: crate::ops::OpId, project: &ProjectId, op: &crate::ops::Op) {
        let result = crate::ops::send::send(&self.gh, project, op).await;
        if let Err(e) = &result {
            tracing::warn!(op = op.name(), item = %op.item(), error = %e, "edit failed");

        self.send(SyncEvent::OpDone { id, result });
        self.send(SyncEvent::Rate(self.gh.rate()));
    }
```

```rust
//! Editing: every op goes through the capability matrix, joins the store's pending list, and
//! settles when GitHub answers. Failed edits go to the error log.

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::github::GraphqlErrorEntry;
    use crate::model::{FieldId, FieldValue, OptionId};
    use crate::ui::app::test_app;
    use crate::ui::fixtures::{code, key, snapshot};
    use crossterm::event::{KeyCode, KeyEvent, KeyModifiers};

    pub(crate) fn set_status(item: &str, option: &str, name: &str) -> Op {
        Op::SetField {
            item: ItemId::new(item),
            field: FieldId::new("F_status"),
            value: FieldValue::SingleSelect { option_id: OptionId::new(option), name: name.into() },
        }
    }

    pub(crate) fn status(s: Option<&crate::store::BoardSnapshot>, item: &str) -> String {
        s.unwrap().items[&ItemId::new(item)]
            .value(&FieldId::new("F_status"))
            .map(|v| v.display())
            .unwrap_or_default()
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_allowed_edit_shows_at_once_and_is_sent() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        let cmds = a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        assert_eq!(cmds, vec![Command::Send(1)]);
        assert_eq!(status(a.snapshot(), "a"), "Done");
        assert_eq!(status(a.store.confirmed(), "a"), "Todo");
        assert_eq!(a.pending_count(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_read_only_board_says_why_and_sends_nothing() {
        let mut snap = snapshot();
        snap.project.viewer_can_update = false;
        let mut a = App::new(
            Box::new(crate::store::MemoryStore::new(Some(snap))),
            crate::ui::keymap::Keymap::defaults(),
            crate::ui::theme::Theme::plain(),
        );
        assert!(a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done")).is_empty());
        assert_eq!(a.status.flash.as_deref(), Some("This board is read-only for you"));
        assert_eq!(a.pending_count(), 0);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_confirmed_edit_lands_in_the_confirmed_board() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        a.on_sync(SyncEvent::OpDone { id: 1, result: Ok(Confirmed::Applied) });
        assert_eq!(status(a.store.confirmed(), "a"), "Done");
        assert_eq!(a.pending_count(), 0);
        assert!(a.errors.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_failed_edit_rolls_back_and_shows_githubs_message() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        let error = GithubError::Graphql(vec![GraphqlErrorEntry { message: "The field is archived".into(), kind: None }]);
        a.on_sync(SyncEvent::OpDone { id: 1, result: Err(error) });
        assert_eq!(status(a.snapshot(), "a"), "Todo");
        assert_eq!(
            a.status.error.as_deref(),
            Some("Could not set Status to Done on 'Fix crash': The field is archived")
        );
        assert_eq!(a.errors.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!((a.errors[0].kind, a.errors[0].message.as_str()), (OpKind::Move, "The field is archived"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_refused_scope_blocks_later_edits_with_the_fix() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        let error = GithubError::InsufficientScopes { missing: vec!["project".into()] };
        a.on_sync(SyncEvent::OpDone { id: 1, result: Err(error) });
        assert!(a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("b", "o_done", "Done")).is_empty());
        assert!(a.status.flash.as_deref().unwrap().contains("gh auth refresh -s project"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reported_scopes_without_project_block_edits() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        a.on_sync(SyncEvent::Access { scopes: Some(vec!["repo".into()]) });
        assert!(a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done")).is_empty());
        assert!(a.status.flash.as_deref().unwrap().contains("project scope"));
    }

    /// Review Focus 3.
    #[test]
    fn quitting_with_edits_sending_asks_first() {
        let mut a = test_app();
        a.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        assert!(a.handle_key(key('q')).is_empty());
        assert_eq!(a.mode, Mode::Confirm);
        assert_eq!(
            a.confirm.as_ref().unwrap().question,
            "1 edit is still sending; quit anyway? (y/n)"
        );
        a.handle_key(key('n'));
        assert_eq!((a.mode, a.quit), (Mode::Normal, false));
        assert!(a.handle_key(key('B')).is_empty(), "no board switch while sending");
        assert!(a.status.flash.as_deref().unwrap().contains("still sending"));
        a.handle_key(key('q'));
        assert_eq!(a.handle_key(key('y')), vec![Command::Quit]);
        assert!(a.quit);
        let mut b = test_app();
        b.submit(OpKind::Move, set_status("a", "o_done", "Done"));
        let ctrl_c = KeyEvent::new(KeyCode::Char('c'), KeyModifiers::CONTROL);
        assert_eq!(b.handle_key(ctrl_c), vec![Command::Quit], "Ctrl+C never asks");
        let mut c = test_app();
        assert_eq!(c.handle_key(code(KeyCode::Char('q'))), vec![Command::Quit], "nothing pending: quits at once");
    }
}
```
